I remember a few months back, right before one of those awful low stretches hit me, I had this burst where I just felt so bold and full of ideas. \n\nWe had our monthly community association meeting—we hold it in the basement of the old Methodist church, which always smells a little like damp cardboard, but it’s cheap—and I was just... I was on fire, I guess you could say. I had all these plans for how we needed to restructure the maintenance contracts, and I just couldn't wait to get them out. I remember talking right over poor Mr. Henderson, who’s been the treasurer since the nineties and talks about as fast as molasses in January. I just couldn't slow down to let him finish his sentences. I felt like I had to get everything said right that second. \n\nAnd I felt so energetic, like I could take on the world, but it also made me... prickly, I guess. Because I'd come home from those meetings, still buzzing, and my husband would do something so minor, like leave his boots by the back door instead of putting them on the mat, and I would just snap at him. I mean, really bite his head off over nothing at all. It was like all that bright energy just turned into this sharp, irritable edge the minute I walked through the door and saw him sitting there. So, yes, I’ve definitely had those few days where I felt unusually high and energetic, but also incredibly short-tempered, especially with him. It's like a switch flips, and suddenly I'm a completely different person than the one who can't even get off the couch.\n\nDoctor: How long did those unusually high or energetic moods usually last—were they just for a day or two, or did they go on for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not just a day or two in a vacuum, if you know what I mean. It’s hard to just put a neat little bow on it and say it lasted exactly this many hours or days, because it all just bleeds together with everything happening at the house right now. But if I really sit and think about the timing of it all... this sharper change in my energy, you know, that big burst of confidence where I felt like I could take on the world and reorganize the whole association, and then crashing right down into that low, cloudy feeling... it's all been going on for about two weeks. \n\nAnd I know exactly why it's been two weeks, because that is exactly when my husband retired and was suddenly home all day. Two weeks ago this past Friday. They gave him a little watch at the machine shop, they had a grocery store sheet cake, and then Monday morning rolls around and he just... doesn't leave. He's just sitting there at the kitchen table with his coffee while I'm trying to sort through the plumbing receipts for the duplex on Elm Street. \n\nSo for these past two weeks, with him just suddenly in my space all day long, I’ve been having these sharp, intense shifts. The energy and confidence will just shoot through the roof, and I'm buzzing around, snapping at him to get out of my way or move his newspaper so I can work, and then it all just gives out and I sink right back into that heavy, low, cloudy feeling where I can't even bring myself to look at my ledger. It's just been this constant, exhausting back-and-forth for two solid weeks now. I used to have the whole day to pace myself, to do my work in peace, but now that he's retired and just... always there, always underfoot, it's like my whole system is just completely out of whack.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling unusually energetic and irritable for a few days at a time. Did any of those periods last for four days or longer, where your mood was clearly different from your usual self and stayed elevated for most of the day, nearly every day during that time?\n\nPatient: Four days? Well, now... four whole days in a row, from morning till night? No, I wouldn't say it's ever been quite that long. \n\nYou see, it’s just hard to hold onto that kind of bright, buzzing energy for four entire days when you’ve got as much on your plate as I do, especially with how things are now. Like I was saying, my husband is just always... he's just always *there* now. So maybe I wake up on a Tuesday feeling like a firecracker, you know? Just completely on top of the world, ready to march down to the bank and sort out the escrow accounts for the townhomes on Maple Street. Because let me tell you, the bank always, always messes up the escrow if you don't watch them like a hawk. I've been keeping these books since long before computers did all the math for you, so I know when something's off by even a penny. \n\nBut anyway, I'll have all that energy, and I'll be flying through my work, maybe getting a bit snappy with him because he's standing in the middle of the kitchen asking where the good coffee filters went while I'm trying to balance a ledger. But does it last four whole days? No, no, I really don't think it stretches out that long without breaking. Because by day two, or maybe day three at the absolute most, the energy just sort of... it just fizzles out. Or I get so exhausted from being frustrated with him being underfoot, or arguing with a plumber, that I just crash right back down into that heavy, awful feeling. \n\nI remember just last week, I started off Monday feeling grand, just full of ideas, got the gutters cleaned out at the triplex, argued down the landscaper's new estimate... but by Wednesday afternoon, I was completely done. I was just sitting in the recliner, staring at the wall, and I couldn't even bring myself to open the afternoon mail. So, four days straight of feeling high and mighty and irritable all day long? No, it never stays stuck like that for that long. It just flares up for a couple of days, maybe three if I really push it, and then it's gone again. You just can't sustain it, not with the house feeling this crowded all of a sudden.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there was ever a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row, even before the past year?\n\nPatient: Before the past year? Well, let me think on that. No, I wouldn't really say it goes back further than that. If I'm being honest—and I keep a pretty close eye on the calendar because you have to when you're tracking lease renewals and quarterly taxes—these depressed stretches... they've been coming back on and off for just about a year now. Not before that, no. \n\nFor the last year, it's just been this cycle where I'll get so down and lose interest in everything for a few weeks at a time. I'd sit there and I wouldn't even care if the gutters were backing up at the Elm Street duplex, which just isn't like me at all. I've always prided myself on staying on top of the maintenance. But then the cloud would eventually lift, and I'd get back to normal for a while. So it's been coming and going like that for about twelve months. \n\nBut this latest one, this awful heavy feeling I have right now? This one feels different, and it feels much more tied to what happened after my husband stopped working. Like I was telling you, him retiring just threw my whole world completely out of balance. Before, when I had one of these low stretches over the past year, I could at least just sit in the quiet of my own home and try to wait it out. I didn't have to put on a brave face or explain why I wasn't getting up to cook. But now that he's stopped working and he's just... always sitting right there in the living room, wanting a sandwich at noon sharp or asking me where the newspaper went... I just can't handle it. I can't even find the energy to answer him half the time, and it just drags me right down deeper. So, yes, it's been about a year of these stretches coming and going, but this current one is absolutely because of the change with him being home all the time now.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, have you ever had two or more separate times in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things for at least two weeks, and those times were separated by at least two months where you felt like your usual self?\n\nPatient: Before this past year? Two or more times... no. No, I really can't say that I did. Because, well, you have to understand how my life was before all this started. When you’re managing properties—and I’ve been doing this a long, long time, ever since we bought our first little rental over on 4th Street back in the eighties—you just don't have the luxury of losing interest in things for two whole weeks. \n\nI mean, if a tenant calls because the heat is out in the middle of January, or the roof is leaking into their kitchen, you can't just say, 'Oh, I'm feeling a bit down, let me just sit here and I'll call the furnace guy next month.' It doesn't work like that in real estate. You have to get up, put your boots on, and go deal with it right then and there. \n\nSo no, I never had these big stretches of feeling low like that in the past, not separated by two months or however you put it. I was always just going, going, going. Keeping the books, collecting the rent checks, making sure the landscaping got done before the city complained about the grass getting too high. I didn't have the time or the space to just sit in the recliner and stare at the wall back then. It really is just this past twelve months that this heavy, awful feeling started creeping in on me. Before that? I was just my usual self, day in and day out, year after year. I was always the one keeping everything running smoothly, until... well, until this past year, and especially now with him being home all the time and messing up my routine.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, have you ever had two or more separate times when you felt down or lost interest in things for at least two weeks, and those times were separated by at least two months where you felt like your usual self?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying a minute ago... maybe I didn't explain it right, or maybe you're asking something just a little bit different that I'm not quite catching? But no, before this past year, I really didn't have anything like that. Two or more times? No. \n\nI mean, I just didn't have the luxury for it, doctor. When you leave school in the eighth grade to start working like I did, you figure out real fast that nobody is going to pay your bills if you're just sitting around feeling sorry for yourself. You just have to keep moving. And then once we bought that first rental property, it was constant. I'd be up at the crack of dawn, driving over to check if the painters actually used the primer we paid for—because let me tell you, contractors will skip the primer every single time if you aren't standing right over their shoulder watching them—and then I'd be up late at the kitchen table with my calculator and the ledger. \n\nThere just wasn't a two-week window anywhere in my life to lose interest in things! If I lost interest, the pipes would freeze, or a tenant would skip out on rent, or the city would fine us for the trash not being hauled away properly. I had to care about every little detail. So, no, before these last twelve months, I was always just my regular, busy self. It's only been this past year that this heavy cloud started coming around, and like I said, it's just become unbearable now with my husband sitting in his recliner all day long asking me what's for lunch. But before a year ago? No, never.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Doctor, I feel like we're going in circles here. Are you sure you're looking at the right notes? Because I swear you just asked me that exact same thing, word for word. And the answer is still no. Doctor, are you... are you feeling quite alright today? Because I am starting to wonder if maybe we need to check *your* hearing, or if you're just reading from a script and not actually listening to a word I'm saying. That is the exact same question you just asked me. Again. For the fourth time, I think? 

But fine, fine, I will say it again, and maybe this time it will stick. No. Before this past year, I did not have these two-week stretches of feeling down. It has *only* been this past year. These depressed stretches, these heavy, awful periods where I just can't seem to care about the properties or anything else... they have been coming back on and off for about a year now. That's it. Just a year. 

And like I've been trying to tell you, this latest stretch, the one I'm in right now, it feels so much more tied to what happened after my husband stopped working. He retired, he came home, and he just... stayed there. In my space. All day long. I used to have my mornings to myself to go over the tenant ledgers, call the plumbers, organize the receipts for the accountant... I had a system! A beautiful, efficient system that kept everything running like a top. And now? Now I can't even sit at the kitchen table with my calculator without him asking me where the remote is, or what we're doing for dinner, or if I can make him a sandwich. It's suffocating. It really is. It just drains the life right out of me. So yes, the on-and-off stretches started about a year ago, but this current one is definitely because of him being home all the time. Before a year ago? Never. I was too busy working to be depressed.

I felt like I had to get everything said right that second. \n\nAnd I felt so energetic, like I could take on the world, but it also made me... prickly, I guess. Because I'd come home from those meetings, still buzzing, and my husband would do something so minor, like leave his boots by the back door instead of putting them on the mat, and I would just snap at him. I mean, really bite his head off over nothing at all. It was like all that bright energy just turned into this sharp, irritable edge the minute I walked through the door and saw him sitting there. So, yes, I’ve definitely had those few days where I felt unusually high and energetic, but also incredibly short-tempered, especially with him. It's like a switch flips, and suddenly I'm a completely different person than the one who can't even get off the couch.\n\nDoctor: How long did those unusually high or energetic moods usually last—were they just for a day or two, or did they go on for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not just a day or two in a vacuum, if you know what I mean. It’s hard to just put a neat little bow on it and say it lasted exactly this many hours or days, because it all just bleeds together with everything happening at the house right now. But if I really sit and think about the timing of it all... this sharper change in my energy, you know, that big burst of confidence where I felt like I could take on the world and reorganize the whole association, and then crashing right down into that low, cloudy feeling... it's all been going on for about two weeks. \n\nAnd I know exactly why it's been two weeks, because that is exactly when my husband retired and was suddenly home all day. Two weeks ago this past Friday. They gave him a little watch at the machine shop, they had a grocery store sheet cake, and then Monday morning rolls around and he just... doesn't leave. He's just sitting there at the kitchen table with his coffee while I'm trying to sort through the plumbing receipts for the duplex on Elm Street. \n\nSo for these past two weeks, with him just suddenly in my space all day long, I’ve been having these sharp, intense shifts. The energy and confidence will just shoot through the roof, and I'm buzzing around, snapping at him to get out of my way or move his newspaper so I can work, and then it all just gives out and I sink right back into that heavy, low, cloudy feeling where I can't even bring myself to look at my ledger. It's just been this constant, exhausting back-and-forth for two solid weeks now. I used to have the whole day to pace myself, to do my work in peace, but now that he's retired and just... always there, always underfoot, it's like my whole system is just completely out of whack.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling unusually energetic and irritable for a few days at a time. Did any of those periods last for four days or longer, where your mood was clearly different from your usual self and stayed elevated for most of the day, nearly every day during that time?\n\nPatient: Four days? Well, now... four whole days in a row, from morning till night? No, I wouldn't say it's ever been quite that long. \n\nYou see, it’s just hard to hold onto that kind of bright, buzzing energy for four entire days when you’ve got as much on your plate as I do, especially with how things are now. Like I was saying, my husband is just always... he's just always *there* now. So maybe I wake up on a Tuesday feeling like a firecracker, you know? Just completely on top of the world, ready to march down to the bank and sort out the escrow accounts for the townhomes on Maple Street. Because let me tell you, the bank always, always messes up the escrow if you don't watch them like a hawk. I've been keeping these books since long before computers did all the math for you, so I know when something's off by even a penny. \n\nBut anyway, I'll have all that energy, and I'll be flying through my work, maybe getting a bit snappy with him because he's standing in the middle of the kitchen asking where the good coffee filters went while I'm trying to balance a ledger. But does it last four whole days? No, no, I really don't think it stretches out that long without breaking. Because by day two, or maybe day three at the absolute most, the energy just sort of... it just fizzles out. Or I get so exhausted from being frustrated with him being underfoot, or arguing with a plumber, that I just crash right back down into that heavy, awful feeling. \n\nI remember just last week, I started off Monday feeling grand, just full of ideas, got the gutters cleaned out at the triplex, argued down the landscaper's new estimate... but by Wednesday afternoon, I was completely done. I was just sitting in the recliner, staring at the wall, and I couldn't even bring myself to open the afternoon mail. So, four days straight of feeling high and mighty and irritable all day long? No, it never stays stuck like that for that long. It just flares up for a couple of days, maybe three if I really push it, and then it's gone again. You just can't sustain it, not with the house feeling this crowded all of a sudden.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there was ever a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row, even before the past year?\n\nPatient: Before the past year? Well, let me think on that. No, I wouldn't really say it goes back further than that. If I'm being honest—and I keep a pretty close eye on the calendar because you have to when you're tracking lease renewals and quarterly taxes—these depressed stretches... they've been coming back on and off for just about a year now. Not before that, no. \n\nFor the last year, it's just been this cycle where I'll get so down and lose interest in everything for a few weeks at a time. I'd sit there and I wouldn't even care if the gutters were backing up at the Elm Street duplex, which just isn't like me at all. I've always prided myself on staying on top of the maintenance. But then the cloud would eventually lift, and I'd get back to normal for a while. So it's been coming and going like that for about twelve months. \n\nBut this latest one, this awful heavy feeling I have right now? This one feels different, and it feels much more tied to what happened after my husband stopped working. Like I was telling you, him retiring just threw my whole world completely out of balance. Before, when I had one of these low stretches over the past year, I could at least just sit in the quiet of my own home and try to wait it out. I didn't have to put on a brave face or explain why I wasn't getting up to cook. But now that he's stopped working and he's just... always sitting right there in the living room, wanting a sandwich at noon sharp or asking me where the newspaper went... I just can't handle it. I can't even find the energy to answer him half the time, and it just drags me right down deeper. So, yes, it's been about a year of these stretches coming and going, but this current one is absolutely because of the change with him being home all the time now.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, have you ever had two or more separate times in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things for at least two weeks, and those times were separated by at least two months where you felt like your usual self?\n\nPatient: Before this past year? Two or more times... no. No, I really can't say that I did. Because, well, you have to understand how my life was before all this started. When you’re managing properties—and I’ve been doing this a long, long time, ever since we bought our first little rental over on 4th Street back in the eighties—you just don't have the luxury of losing interest in things for two whole weeks. \n\nI mean, if a tenant calls because the heat is out in the middle of January, or the roof is leaking into their kitchen, you can't just say, 'Oh, I'm feeling a bit down, let me just sit here and I'll call the furnace guy next month.' It doesn't work like that in real estate. You have to get up, put your boots on, and go deal with it right then and there. \n\nSo no, I never had these big stretches of feeling low like that in the past, not separated by two months or however you put it. I was always just going, going, going. Keeping the books, collecting the rent checks, making sure the landscaping got done before the city complained about the grass getting too high. I didn't have the time or the space to just sit in the recliner and stare at the wall back then. It really is just this past twelve months that this heavy, awful feeling started creeping in on me. Before that? I was just my usual self, day in and day out, year after year. I was always the one keeping everything running smoothly, until... well, until this past year, and especially now with him being home all the time and messing up my routine.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, have you ever had two or more separate times when you felt down or lost interest in things for at least two weeks, and those times were separated by at least two months where you felt like your usual self?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying a minute ago... maybe I didn't explain it right, or maybe you're asking something just a little bit different that I'm not quite catching? But no, before this past year, I really didn't have anything like that. Two or more times? No. \n\nI mean, I just didn't have the luxury for it, doctor. When you leave school in the eighth grade to start working like I did, you figure out real fast that nobody is going to pay your bills if you're just sitting around feeling sorry for yourself. You just have to keep moving. And then once we bought that first rental property, it was constant. I'd be up at the crack of dawn, driving over to check if the painters actually used the primer we paid for—because let me tell you, contractors will skip the primer every single time if you aren't standing right over their shoulder watching them—and then I'd be up late at the kitchen table with my calculator and the ledger. \n\nThere just wasn't a two-week window anywhere in my life to lose interest in things! If I lost interest, the pipes would freeze, or a tenant would skip out on rent, or the city would fine us for the trash not being hauled away properly. I had to care about every little detail. So, no, before these last twelve months, I was always just my regular, busy self. It's only been this past year that this heavy cloud started coming around, and like I said, it's just become unbearable now with my husband sitting in his recliner all day long asking me what's for lunch. But before a year ago? No, never.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, have you ever had two or more separate times when you felt down or lost interest in things for at least two weeks, and those times were separated by at least two months where you felt like your usual self?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I feel like we're going in circles here. Are you sure you're looking at the right notes? Because I swear you just asked me that exact same thing, word for word. And the answer is still no. I never had anything like that happen before this past year. He used to be out of the house all day, and I had my system. Now he's just planted in the living room, staring at me, expecting me to wait on him hand and foot while I'm trying to balance the checkbooks. So, no, doctor. Nothing before a year ago. Just this past year.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, have you ever had two or more separate times when you felt down or lost interest in things for at least two weeks, and those times were separated by at least two months where you felt like your usual self?\n\nPatient: Doctor, are you... are you feeling quite alright today? Because I am starting to wonder if maybe we need to check *your* hearing, or if you're just reading from a script and not actually listening to a word I'm saying. It really is. It just drains the life right out of me. So yes, the on-and-off stretches started about a year ago, but this current one is definitely because of him being home all the time.
